Dated 2023-04-12, it looks like a Stage 2 sample with the C&C address set to 127.0.0.1, which leads us to believe that it was used for testing. It also includes limited functionality that currently is available in Stage 3 samples (only listening for OS commands (executed with  exec) ), which reinforces our assumption that the malware was in de","\u002Fblog\u002Flabs\u002Finfected-minecraft-mods-lead-to-multi-stage-multi-platform-infostealer-malware\u002F","6a6a1b88a89a0404d6eab7c0","Fake Xeno Roblox Cheats Deliver Powerful Java Stealer Through Discord and Forums","fake-xeno-roblox-discord-executor","https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002FChatGPT-Image-Aug-3--2026--02_23_41-PM-1.jpg","2026-08-03T15:51:24.000+03:00","\u003Cp\u003E\u003Cem\u003EA malware campaign disguised as an “undetected” version of the Xeno Roblox script executor is directly affecting players looking to download a legitimate tool.\u003C\u002Fem\u003E\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EPromoted through various gaming forums and Discord communities, the fake cheat launches a multi-stage Java infection chain built to stay hidden in plain sight. Its components imitate real Xeno files, use Windows-style names and hide inside trusted-looking directories, which includes a folder associated with Xbox Game Bar, formerly Microsoft GameDVR.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe final payload goes far beyond conventional credential theft. It can steal browser cookies, Discord, Roblox and Minecraft accounts, cryptocurrency-wallet data and payment information.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EUnlike the more generic stealers, this one can also record keystrokes, access the webcam, stream the victim’s desktop, manipulate files, run PowerShell commands and give attackers interactive control of the infected computer.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003ENewly identified command-and-control infrastructure and expanded functionality suggest the malware, previously documented as Powercat, remains under active development.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe campaign is particularly worrying because Roblox-related cheats can attract children and teenagers, potentially exposing accounts, private conversations, webcam images and financial information stored on shared family computers.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Ch2 id=\"key-findings\"\u003EKey findings\u003C\u002Fh2\u003E\u003Cul\u003E\u003Cli\u003EAttackers distribute the malware as an undetected version of the Xeno Roblox script executor through gaming forums and Discord communities.\u003C\u002Fli\u003E\u003Cli\u003EThe campaign uses a multi-stage Java infection chain with files, directories and persistence mechanisms that imitate legitimate Windows and gaming components.\u003C\u002Fli\u003E\u003Cli\u003EThe final payload combines information theft, surveillance, persistence, remote access, file manipulation and command execution.\u003C\u002Fli\u003E\u003Cli\u003EIts targets include Discord, Roblox and Minecraft accounts, browser data, cryptocurrency wallets and payment-related tokens.\u003C\u002Fli\u003E\u003Cli\u003EResearchers identified new command-and-control infrastructure and additional capabilities, suggesting continued development.\u003C\u002Fli\u003E\u003Cli\u003ESecurity insights show that users have been affected since the beginning of the year, with activity rising sharply in the second half of March.\u003C\u002Fli\u003E\u003C\u002Ful\u003E\u003Ch1 id=\"introduction\"\u003EIntroduction\u003C\u002Fh1\u003E\u003Cp\u003EGaming communities offer threat actors a fruitful environment for reaching potential victims. Players frequently exchange mods, scripts, and unofficial tools through forums, Discord servers and file-sharing websites.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EAttackers are all too aware of gamers’ habits. They can exploit this behavior by disguising malware as cheats or utilities that promise exclusive features, improved performance, or the ability to evade anti-cheat systems.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EBitdefender security researchers have identified a malware campaign targeting players searching for cheats for games such as Roblox. The campaign impersonates Xeno, a popular Roblox script executor used to automate actions and run custom scripts.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EBecause such tools are frequently detected or blocked by the game client, versions advertised as “undetected” can be particularly attractive to users seeking to bypass these restrictions.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe malicious packages are promoted through forums and Discord communities, either directly by the operators or through compromised and impersonated accounts.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThroughout the infection chain, the malware tries to maintain a veneer of legitimacy. Its components imitate files from a Xeno installation, while later stages use Windows-like DLL names, trusted-looking directories and display-related persistence entries.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe final malware stage is a sophisticated stealer and remote access trojan that uses anti-analysis and anti-sandboxing techniques, which means that it’s trying hard not to be analyzed by security researchers.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EIt can steal browser cookies, personal accounts such as Discord, Roblox and Minecraft, and payment-related information that could lead to financial damage.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThreatLocker researchers previously \u003Ca href=\"https:\u002F\u002Fwww.threatlocker.com\u002Fblog\u002Fpowercat-malware-campaign-fake-game-cheats-deliver-infostealer-targeting-discord-roblox-and-crypto-wallets\"\u003Edocumented\u003C\u002Fa\u003E this campaign under the name Powercat. Our insights revealed additional command-and-control domains and capabilities, which suggests that the malware remains under active development and continues to be used in cybercriminal operations.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe campaign has affected users since the beginning of the year. Activity increased significantly during the second half of March and has since maintained a relatively consistent infection rate.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002Fkillchain_java.drawio.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"561\" height=\"602\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 1. Java stealer killchain\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Ch2 id=\"initial-access-fake-xeno-cheats-and-stage-1-execution\"\u003EInitial Access: Fake Xeno Cheats and Stage 1 Execution\u003C\u002Fh2\u003E\u003Cp\u003EFor initial access, users are tricked into downloading fake game cheats, such as Xeno, a widely used cheat software for Roblox. The software packages often come either in the form of archives containing extraction and execution instructions or as self-extracting archives that automatically unpack and prepare the environment.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThese archives use a directory structure that resembles a legitimate Xeno installation. File names are chosen to make the package look convincing to users expecting Roblox cheat software. Some files are indeed LUA scripts taken from a Xeno installation, while others, such as RbxAnalytics.png, are only small 10-byte files containing junk data.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-A1A65453-44F4-4F08-8B22-D71DB67EB39E--20260708-094739-1.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"333\" height=\"123\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 2. Mimicking a Xeno installation\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe user is then told to run the main entry point located at \u003Cem\u003E%LOCALAPPDATA%\\Xeno\\workspace\\cache\\xeno.exe\u003C\u002Fem\u003E, believing it will launch the expected game cheat. In reality, this is not the legitimate Xeno binary, but the first stage of the malware masquerading as it.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EFirst, xeno.exe checks whether a Java executable exists at %LOCALAPPDATA%\\Java\\jre\\bin\\javaw.exe. If the file is missing, it extracts a Java Runtime Environment to that path from an archive named instance.exe, using an embedded PowerShell command.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cpre\u003E\u003Ccode class=\"language-bash\"\u003Epowershell -NoProfile -WindowStyle Hidden -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -Command \\\"Add-Type -AssemblyName System.IO.Compression.FileSystem;[System.IO.Compression.ZipFile]::ExtractToDirectory(...)\u003C\u002Fcode\u003E\u003C\u002Fpre\u003E\n\u003Cp\u003ENext, xeno.exe reads the contents of \u003Cem\u003EXenoIcon.jpg\u003C\u002Fem\u003E, which contains the keys required by the second stage to validate its execution with the C2 server. Finally, it launches javaw.exe to execute a JAR file masquerading as \u003Cem\u003Edecompiler.exe\u003C\u002Fem\u003E, passing the contents of XenoIcon.jpg as command-line arguments.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Ch2 id=\"stage-2-decompilerexe-a-jar-disguised-as-a-windows-executable\"\u003EStage 2: decompiler.exe, a JAR Disguised as a Windows Executable\u003C\u002Fh2\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe decompiler.exe file is a JAR file masquerading as a Windows executable. Its Java bytecode is obfuscated with the demo version of Allatori, a legitimate commercial obfuscation tool that the malware authors have abused. This can be at least partially deobfuscated, although the flow obfuscation is not fully reversible.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe class and variable names are also characteristic of Allatori, making the decompiled code harder to read. The main function begins by printing a banner to the console.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-FF1DD0FD-8D2E-4865-9B48-404CE9B56DF9--20260709-063310-1.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"385\" height=\"397\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 3. Allatori banner\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003EIf the two required arguments are passed to the application, it starts collecting information about the environment in which it is running.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe malware looks for traces of debugging, artifacts that indicate sandbox execution, and information needed to validate the JAR execution.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EAfter gathering these details, it sends a POST request to the C2 server containing the collected flag values.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThese values are used to register the victim machine and determine whether it should receive additional payloads.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-570BB6C5-ABCA-4E30-80D6-3BD4B77C4C7E--20260709-065550.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"646\" height=\"199\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-570BB6C5-ABCA-4E30-80D6-3BD4B77C4C7E--20260709-065550.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-570BB6C5-ABCA-4E30-80D6-3BD4B77C4C7E--20260709-065550.png 646w\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Ca href=\"\"\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 4. Environment checking\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Fa\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003EFor debug detection, the code checks runtime arguments such as \u003Cem\u003E-xdebug\u003C\u002Fem\u003E, \u003Cem\u003Esuspend=\u003C\u002Fem\u003E, and \u003Cem\u003Ejdwp\u003C\u002Fem\u003E, as well as modules appearing in stack traces, including \u003Cem\u003Eintellij\u003C\u002Fem\u003E, \u003Cem\u003Eeclipse.jdt\u003C\u002Fem\u003E, \u003Cem\u003Enetbeans\u003C\u002Fem\u003E, and \u003Cem\u003Ebytecodeviewer\u003C\u002Fem\u003E. If any of these strings are present, the code returns zero, causing the final flag value to fall below the threshold expected by the server.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-7A4D2C77-1448-4A8E-8E71-B631E4339C08--20260709-073147.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"814\" height=\"304\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-7A4D2C77-1448-4A8E-8E71-B631E4339C08--20260709-073147.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-7A4D2C77-1448-4A8E-8E71-B631E4339C08--20260709-073147.png 814w\" sizes=\"(min-width: 720px) 720px\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 5. Checking current thread’s stack trace\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-BBC64CD0-7B83-442D-B708-4FE3FEA896D2--20260709-073346.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"673\" height=\"77\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-BBC64CD0-7B83-442D-B708-4FE3FEA896D2--20260709-073346.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-BBC64CD0-7B83-442D-B708-4FE3FEA896D2--20260709-073346.png 673w\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 6. Constructing the final value of the debug flag\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003ESandbox detection is performed through several checks. The malware verifies whether the disk partition size exceeds 20 GB, looks for well-known MAC addresses associated with emulated network adapters, checks the registry and WMI for virtual machine artifacts, and iterates over running processes to detect monitoring tools commonly used in automated analysis environments.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002Fimage-2.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"229\" height=\"163\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 7. Virtualization vendors checked\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-3010E6A5-E7FF-4A59-88FC-15B4FDD62E4F--20260709-074151.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"350\" height=\"344\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 8. Monitoring processes checked\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe function responsible for communicating with the C2 server receives a message as a parameter, creates a JSON object from it, opens a connection to \u003Cstrong\u003Ehxxps:\u002F\u002Fsolthere[.]net\u002Fjustacoolkat10\u003C\u002Fstrong\u003E, and sends the JSON object through a POST request.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-8070BC9D-F8BE-4732-BC96-B3D4ABCB9DE3--20260709-075203.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"741\" height=\"344\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-8070BC9D-F8BE-4732-BC96-B3D4ABCB9DE3--20260709-075203.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-8070BC9D-F8BE-4732-BC96-B3D4ABCB9DE3--20260709-075203.png 741w\" sizes=\"(min-width: 720px) 720px\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 9. Post request to C2\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003EAfter this initial logging, the code calls another function that connects to the C2 server through a different endpoint and sends the key received through the command-line arguments for validation.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe URL used for payload retrieval is \u003Cstrong\u003Ehxxps:\u002F\u002Fsolthere[.]net\u002Fapi\u002Fv1\u002Fredeem\u003C\u002Fstrong\u003E. The key is encrypted with AES, and the resulting buffer is Base64-encoded before being sent in the POST request body.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-86A13ADB-11AE-4382-9DAE-65AED462C29A--20260709-080001.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"690\" height=\"163\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-86A13ADB-11AE-4382-9DAE-65AED462C29A--20260709-080001.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-86A13ADB-11AE-4382-9DAE-65AED462C29A--20260709-080001.png 690w\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 10. Redeem API\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-64E4A3AB-A34A-49AA-9E55-C1D465A48A83--20260709-080951.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"812\" height=\"414\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-64E4A3AB-A34A-49AA-9E55-C1D465A48A83--20260709-080951.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-64E4A3AB-A34A-49AA-9E55-C1D465A48A83--20260709-080951.png 812w\" sizes=\"(min-width: 720px) 720px\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 11. Communicating with the API of the C2\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe retrieved payload is then loaded using the \u003Cem\u003EloadClass\u003C\u002Fem\u003E method. Its entry point is identified by iterating through all methods of the received object.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EOnce the entry point is found, it is invoked through \u003Cem\u003Emethod.invoke()\u003C\u002Fem\u003E. This payload then downloads and writes the third stage to %LOCALAPPDATA%\\Microsoft\\GameDVR.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003ETo execute it, the malware launches a new javaw.exe process and passes the path of the JAR file as an argument.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Ch2 id=\"stage-3-a-java-stealer-masquerading-as-a-windows-dll\"\u003EStage 3: A Java Stealer Masquerading as a Windows DLL\u003C\u002Fh2\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe third stage is a Java-based stealer and surveillance malware that can collect personal information from browsers, Discord, and games. It can also collect screenshots and webcam footage, stream the victim’s desktop, and monitor keyboard and mouse activity.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EIn addition, it can download and upload files, execute commands through PowerShell, and open an interactive shell for hands-on-keyboard access, allowing full system takeover.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe JAR file masquerades as a seemingly legitimate DLL located in %LOCALAPPDATA%\\Microsoft\\GameDVR. The possible DLL names mimic legitimate Windows DLLs that normally reside in the Windows directory.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-31700A5E-EF54-445C-877C-6BA3D8ADE4F0--20260709-093052.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"538\" height=\"127\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 12. Possible masquerading DLL names\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003EUpon execution, the malware checks the environment in a similar fashion to the second stage. However, instead of sending the collected information to the C2 server, it terminates if a sandbox is detected.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe malware also creates a local log file named \u003Cem\u003E-ntcache\u003C\u002Fem\u003E in the current user’s home directory, where it records execution progress with timestamps.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-D0966235-CDFC-4325-BF0C-6DE5D0877074--20260709-093937.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"968\" height=\"489\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-D0966235-CDFC-4325-BF0C-6DE5D0877074--20260709-093937.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-D0966235-CDFC-4325-BF0C-6DE5D0877074--20260709-093937.png 968w\" sizes=\"(min-width: 720px) 720px\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 13. Logging function\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003EAfter that, the malware attempts to move execution to a CMSTP process with elevated privileges. If privilege escalation succeeds, the low-privilege javaw.exe process terminates. If privilege escalation fails, execution continues.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe subsequent malware flow appears to be the same in both elevated and non-elevated contexts.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-1A951009-6C57-4EA5-94B6-BB95B6742ABB--20260709-094913.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"1768\" height=\"519\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-1A951009-6C57-4EA5-94B6-BB95B6742ABB--20260709-094913.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw1000\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-1A951009-6C57-4EA5-94B6-BB95B6742ABB--20260709-094913.png 1000w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw1600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-1A951009-6C57-4EA5-94B6-BB95B6742ABB--20260709-094913.png 1600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-1A951009-6C57-4EA5-94B6-BB95B6742ABB--20260709-094913.png 1768w\" sizes=\"(min-width: 720px) 720px\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 14. Privilege escalation via CMSTP\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe malware then contacts two online services that can identify IP addresses (ipapi[.]co and ipwho[.]is) to obtain geolocation information about the victim. This information is later used during the C2 connection process to generate a unique identifier based on the system’s hardware profile.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EPersistence is established by adding the command line that runs the JAR file to the Software\\Microsoft\\Windows\\CurrentVersion\\Run registry key under the name Display Calibration.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-1D9F9688-6A65-4C96-9032-48A7DB92390C--20260709-095631.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"1393\" height=\"330\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-1D9F9688-6A65-4C96-9032-48A7DB92390C--20260709-095631.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw1000\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-1D9F9688-6A65-4C96-9032-48A7DB92390C--20260709-095631.png 1000w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-1D9F9688-6A65-4C96-9032-48A7DB92390C--20260709-095631.png 1393w\" sizes=\"(min-width: 720px) 720px\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 15. Persistence via Run key\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe malware then enables the Run entry by setting Software\\Microsoft\\Windows\\CurrentVersion\\Explorer\\StartupApproved\\Run for Display Calibration to the value 2.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-4A66EF79-300E-4387-8F6B-2B4716D18155--20260709-095911.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"1449\" height=\"179\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-4A66EF79-300E-4387-8F6B-2B4716D18155--20260709-095911.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw1000\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-4A66EF79-300E-4387-8F6B-2B4716D18155--20260709-095911.png 1000w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-4A66EF79-300E-4387-8F6B-2B4716D18155--20260709-095911.png 1449w\" sizes=\"(min-width: 720px) 720px\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 16. Enabling persistence\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003EOnce persistence is enabled, it starts two threads responsible for collecting and sending crypto wallet data stolen from Exodus wallets. It then calls the function that creates a WebSocket connection to the C2 server.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe server URI is generated at runtime by calculating the SHA-256 hash of the string \u003Cem\u003Eprint\u003C\u002Fem\u003E, removing the last character, and appending \u003Cem\u003E.xyz\u003C\u002Fem\u003E.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThus, the resulting C2 address is \u003Cstrong\u003Ece953a0eb08246617b7f849486c4b26a7af37e9d2e8f0e13b3ae1bf0da8a70a[.]xyz\u003C\u002Fstrong\u003E. The management server then assigns a subdomain to the client and responds with the string needed to build the full URI. The client can only connect to the C2 through that URI for the active session.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EOnce the connection is established, the server can send two types of data over the socket. The first type is an updated JAR file. The received bytes are written to %LOCALAPPDATA%\\Microsoft\\GameDVR using one of the DLL-like names from the malware’s list of possible masquerading filenames.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe new version is then executed using the \u003Cem\u003Eexec \u003C\u002Fem\u003Emethod, while the old version terminates by calling \u003Cem\u003Ehalt\u003C\u002Fem\u003E.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-0A6B86C2-EB20-4E2E-A36A-6AAC3CF00C87--20260709-115901.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"1151\" height=\"345\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-0A6B86C2-EB20-4E2E-A36A-6AAC3CF00C87--20260709-115901.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw1000\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-0A6B86C2-EB20-4E2E-A36A-6AAC3CF00C87--20260709-115901.png 1000w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-0A6B86C2-EB20-4E2E-A36A-6AAC3CF00C87--20260709-115901.png 1151w\" sizes=\"(min-width: 720px) 720px\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 17. JAR update mechanism\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe second response type is an encrypted string. Once decrypted, it contains a JSON object with a task identifier and its parameters. The task identifier acts as an index in an array of possible actions.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EBy analyzing all functions in this array, we uncovered the malware’s capabilities.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-D86CD82B-6966-4F76-8E27-C3629047356E--20260709-122940.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"466\" height=\"487\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 18. Possible actions\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThese commands can be grouped into several broader categories: connection management, surveillance, file system operations, personal information theft, shell access, and command execution.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003E\u003Cstrong\u003EConnection management\u003C\u002Fstrong\u003E actions include performing a handshake with a secret string and validating that the client is communicating over the expected socket. The server can also instruct the client to open a new connection on a new socket.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003E\u003Cstrong\u003EFile system commands\u003C\u002Fstrong\u003E include standard directory listing, upload, download, and rename operations. \u003Cstrong\u003ECommand execution\u003C\u002Fstrong\u003E is performed by launching a PowerShell process that executes a Base64-encoded command received as a parameter.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe \u003Cstrong\u003Einteractive shell\u003C\u002Fstrong\u003E is implemented in a similar way, using a PowerShell process whose input and output are redirected to the Java process, which then forwards the buffers to the C2 server.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Ch3 id=\"surveillance-capabilities\"\u003ESurveillance Capabilities\u003C\u002Fh3\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe surveillance command set includes keylogging, mouse movement logging, screenshot capture, display streaming, and webcam capture.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EWebcam capture is performed by interacting with COM objects related to DirectShow. The captured buffers are then streamed to the C2 server.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-116D7363-3F5C-4B9D-8D04-0C86867BCEFA--20260709-143158.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"938\" height=\"851\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-116D7363-3F5C-4B9D-8D04-0C86867BCEFA--20260709-143158.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-116D7363-3F5C-4B9D-8D04-0C86867BCEFA--20260709-143158.png 938w\" sizes=\"(min-width: 720px) 720px\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 19. DirectShow-related GUIDs\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003EDisplay monitoring can be performed in two ways: the malware can either capture individual screenshots using the \u003Cem\u003EscreenshotCapture \u003C\u002Fem\u003Ecommand or enable a streaming mode that captures a screenshot every 500 milliseconds and continuously sends the images to the C2 server, effectively creating a live feed.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe server can also request a list of connected displays in order to choose which one to capture.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-E8B57B90-879C-49B4-89AE-384A7452B376--20260709-144455.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"1065\" height=\"170\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-E8B57B90-879C-49B4-89AE-384A7452B376--20260709-144455.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw1000\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-E8B57B90-879C-49B4-89AE-384A7452B376--20260709-144455.png 1000w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-E8B57B90-879C-49B4-89AE-384A7452B376--20260709-144455.png 1065w\" sizes=\"(min-width: 720px) 720px\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 20. Streaming screenshots every 500 milliseconds\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003EMouse and keyboard logging are implemented through \u003Cem\u003EWinuser \u003C\u002Fem\u003Efrom the Java Native Access API. This allows the malware to interface with Windows functions from \u003Cem\u003EUser32 \u003C\u002Fem\u003Eand register hooks for mouse and keyboard events.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-0158CE95-ABF2-4BEB-A372-1F1C14CE925A--20260709-144743.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"344\" height=\"259\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 12. Mouse events logged\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-C9B8B546-A104-41D5-9360-8BC6ACE7810F--20260709-150005.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"483\" height=\"810\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 22. Strings mapped to key codes\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Ch3 id=\"personal-information-theft\"\u003EPersonal Information Theft\u003C\u002Fh3\u003E\u003Cp\u003EPersonal information theft begins with the malware gathering information about potentially interesting software installed on the victim’s system. This allows the operators to adapt their strategy and prioritize which data to steal. \u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe function we named \u003Cem\u003EmapInterestingSoftware \u003C\u002Fem\u003Echecks standard installation paths for various applications and stores the results in a HashMap. Once the checks are complete, the contents of the HashMap are sent to the C2 server.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-8AD5A219-80D5-4961-BB51-1AA833A0C7C1--20260709-150733.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"914\" height=\"222\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-8AD5A219-80D5-4961-BB51-1AA833A0C7C1--20260709-150733.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-8AD5A219-80D5-4961-BB51-1AA833A0C7C1--20260709-150733.png 914w\" sizes=\"(min-width: 720px) 720px\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 23. Example software mapping\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe checked software includes crypto wallets such as:\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cul\u003E\n\u003Cli\u003EExodus, Atomic, Cake Wallet, SafePal, TronWallet, and Monero Wallet\u003C\u002Fli\u003E\n\u003Cli\u003ESoftware development tools such as Visual Studio, Git, JetBrains tools, and Python IDLE\u003C\u002Fli\u003E\n\u003Cli\u003EGame launchers such as Steam, Rockstar Games Launcher, Battle.net, Epic Games Launcher, and Riot Client\u003C\u002Fli\u003E\n\u003Cli\u003ERoblox and Minecraft installations\u003C\u002Fli\u003E\n\u003Cli\u003EVPN software such as NordVPN, Mullvad VPN, ExpressVPN, and Surfshark\u003C\u002Fli\u003E\n\u003Cli\u003EMessaging software such as Telegram, Snapchat, WhatsApp, and Discord\u003C\u002Fli\u003E\n\u003C\u002Ful\u003E\n\u003Cp\u003EAfter this initial discovery phase, the stealer uses dedicated functions for some of its main targets.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe stealer implements one wallet-specific attack against Exodus cryptocurrency wallets.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThis logic is handled by the two threads instantiated by the main function. The first thread checks whether Exodus version 26.1.5 is installed on the system. If the wallet is present, the malware extracts the app.asar file, injects two small JavaScript snippets into the application’s files and repacks them.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EOne of the scripts modifies the wallet’s sandboxing and context isolation configuration, while the other logs wallet-related buffers to a file named \u003Cem\u003ESquirrelInteractive.bin\u003C\u002Fem\u003E in the user’s home directory.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-AAC6D5A2-145A-48CB-8627-F0D7B56D46FB--20260710-063425.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"952\" height=\"216\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-AAC6D5A2-145A-48CB-8627-F0D7B56D46FB--20260710-063425.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-AAC6D5A2-145A-48CB-8627-F0D7B56D46FB--20260710-063425.png 952w\" sizes=\"(min-width: 720px) 720px\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 24. Validating if Exodus 26.1.5 is installed\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-F808F7F6-0AA1-4463-945E-EAB27D0A221E--20260710-063837.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"967\" height=\"95\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-F808F7F6-0AA1-4463-945E-EAB27D0A221E--20260710-063837.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-F808F7F6-0AA1-4463-945E-EAB27D0A221E--20260710-063837.png 967w\" sizes=\"(min-width: 720px) 720px\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 25. JavaScript injection in Exodus files\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThis approach suggests that the malware is not simply searching for static wallet files, but rather actively tampering with the local Exodus application to expose sensitive runtime data. The second thread periodically checks for the presence of \u003Cem\u003ESquirrelInteractive.bin\u003C\u002Fem\u003E.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EIf the file exists, it parses the logged contents, extracts valid tokens, and sends them to the C2 server.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-DE0AFCEB-D98B-47B7-B768-213A082B2355--20260710-064105.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"967\" height=\"400\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-DE0AFCEB-D98B-47B7-B768-213A082B2355--20260710-064105.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-DE0AFCEB-D98B-47B7-B768-213A082B2355--20260710-064105.png 967w\" sizes=\"(min-width: 720px) 720px\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 26. Wallet Data collector\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003EFor browser collection, the malware defines custom classes for each supported browser, including specific paths where potentially valuable information is stored, such as cookies and user data.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe browsers recognized by the malware are Brave Browser, Chrome, Edge, Opera, Opera GX, and Vivaldi.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-E47E50E7-C197-4044-8A9E-C603EE977ED0--20260709-151912.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"766\" height=\"405\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-E47E50E7-C197-4044-8A9E-C603EE977ED0--20260709-151912.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-E47E50E7-C197-4044-8A9E-C603EE977ED0--20260709-151912.png 766w\" sizes=\"(min-width: 720px) 720px\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 27. Brave Browser definitions\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003EDiscord token theft is performed in two steps. First, the malware checks browser cookies for saved tokens associated with discord[.]com and extracts information stored in browser SQLite databases.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EIt then uses the Discord API to retrieve information about the logged-in account, including stored payment methods.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-994720C0-2D09-4965-9D59-42BFF40BC4B1--20260709-152920.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"1353\" height=\"867\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-994720C0-2D09-4965-9D59-42BFF40BC4B1--20260709-152920.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw1000\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-994720C0-2D09-4965-9D59-42BFF40BC4B1--20260709-152920.png 1000w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-994720C0-2D09-4965-9D59-42BFF40BC4B1--20260709-152920.png 1353w\" sizes=\"(min-width: 720px) 720px\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 28. Discord account retrieval\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003ERoblox and Minecraft cookies are also processed to retrieve account and login information. For Minecraft, the malware can identify multiple launchers and extract sensitive information stored by each of them.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe known launchers are Feather, Lunar, Meteor, Modrinth, Prism, and the official Minecraft launcher.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-3812DA1D-A4F9-49B2-9CE2-FB3D8A9270B7--20260709-153221.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"799\" height=\"380\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-3812DA1D-A4F9-49B2-9CE2-FB3D8A9270B7--20260709-153221.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-3812DA1D-A4F9-49B2-9CE2-FB3D8A9270B7--20260709-153221.png 799w\" sizes=\"(min-width: 720px) 720px\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 29. Roblox cookie parsing\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-AEAAB802-4D7C-4E85-9A6F-717BBD361401--20260709-153329.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"777\" height=\"184\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-AEAAB802-4D7C-4E85-9A6F-717BBD361401--20260709-153329.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002F-AEAAB802-4D7C-4E85-9A6F-717BBD361401--20260709-153329.png 777w\" sizes=\"(min-width: 720px) 720px\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 30. Minecraft cookie parsing\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Cp\u003EFinally, the malware can extract tokens from Microsoft Store logins with the goal of stealing stored payment information. It locates .tbres files in the Token Broker or Cache directories and searches their contents for patterns that indicate the beginning of a token.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EAny valid token found is sent to the C2 server.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cfigure class=\"kg-card kg-image-card kg-card-hascaption\"\u003E\u003Cimg src=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002Fimage-3.png\" class=\"kg-image\" alt=\"\" loading=\"lazy\" width=\"1186\" height=\"507\" srcset=\"https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw600\u002F2026\u002F08\u002Fimage-3.png 600w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002Fsize\u002Fw1000\u002F2026\u002F08\u002Fimage-3.png 1000w, https:\u002F\u002Fblogapp.bitdefender.com\u002Flabs\u002Fcontent\u002Fimages\u002F2026\u002F08\u002Fimage-3.png 1186w\" sizes=\"(min-width: 720px) 720px\"\u003E\u003Cfigcaption\u003E\u003Cspan style=\"white-space: pre-wrap;\"\u003EFig. 31. Microsoft Store token theft\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigcaption\u003E\u003C\u002Ffigure\u003E\u003Ch1 id=\"impact-on-victims\"\u003EImpact on Victims\u003C\u002Fh1\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe malware can severely impact victims’ privacy because it gives attackers access to both stored personal information and real-time activity.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EIn addition to stealing browser cookies, account credentials, cryptocurrency-wallet data, and payment-related tokens, it can record keystrokes and mouse activity, capture screenshots, stream the desktop, access the webcam, and remotely browse or modify files.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThis combination enables attackers to observe private conversations, collect sensitive images and documents, impersonate victims, take over online accounts, and possibly inflict financial loss.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EIts remote-access and command-execution capabilities also mean that the compromise can continue beyond the initial theft of information, which can lead to data destruction or allow operators to use the infected system in other cyber-criminal activities.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Ch1 id=\"conclusions\"\u003EConclusions\u003C\u002Fh1\u003E\u003Cp\u003EGaming-related lures remain effective because they exploit users’ interest in gaining an advantage, accessing restricted functionality, or avoiding anti-cheat detection.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EIn this campaign, attackers used a convincing imitation of the Xeno Roblox script executor and promoted it through channels commonly used by gaming communities. Familiar filenames, copied scripts, legitimate-looking directories, and Windows-related persistence names helped the infection chain appear credible while concealing its malicious purpose.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EThe delivered malware is considerably more capable than a typical credential stealer. Its final stage combines browser and account theft with cryptocurrency-wallet targeting, payment-token collection, keylogging, screenshots, webcam access, desktop streaming, file operations, PowerShell command execution, and interactive shell access.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EPersistence and self-update functionality allow the operators to maintain control over infected systems and deploy new versions, while newly observed infrastructure and capabilities indicate that the campaign remains active and continues to evolve.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EUsers can reduce their exposure by combining effective security controls with cautious online behavior.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EUpdated endpoint protection, reputation-based blocking, application-control policies, multi-factor authentication, and restrictions on software execution can prevent or limit many stages of the attack.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003EHowever, the most effective first line of defense remains avoiding unofficial cheats and executors, particularly those distributed through untrusted websites, archives, forums, or unsolicited Discord messages.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Cp\u003ERecognizing these lures before execution can prevent account theft, financial loss, and complete system compromise. Discussing common gaming-related scams with younger users can also significantly reduce the risk of infection.\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\u003Ch1 payloads\u003Co:p\u003E\u003C\u002Fo:p\u003E\u003C\u002Fspan\u003E\u003C\u002Fp\u003E\n  \u003C\u002Ftd\u003E\n \u003C\u002Ftr\u003E\n \u003Ctr style=\"font-family:&quot;Aptos&quot;,sans-serif;mso-ascii-theme-font:minor-latin;\n
